    Reflections — runnable proof-engineering demonstrations #

    This library collects the self-contained (core Lean, no L4YAML import) runnable illustrations of the proof-engineering principles documented as numbered Reflections in Blueprint/08-initiative-4-intrinsic-foundations.md. They are kept separate from the behavioural L4YAML test suites (parsing/emitting/round-trip) because they assert methodology, not library behaviour: each is a toy model whose #guard/#guard_msgs/#eval/by decide checks fail the build if the principle's positive/negative witnesses ever drift.

    Each module names the Blueprint Reflection it illustrates: The list below is grouped by the same themes as the Blueprint's Reflections section, numerically ascending within each theme. It is PARTIAL -- only the import lines above are load-bearing, so a demo without a bullet is not a defect.

    Lean 4 mechanics & build hygiene #

    Tactics & elaboration (Reflections 592, 593, 596, 611, 613) #

    • LoopPushPointwise — Reflection 592 (element-value join for parseFlow{Sequence,Mapping}Loop_push_pointwise (SS5.10.5). For each index j < result.1.size the loop makes at least one sub-call step that PUSHES v = result.1[j]! into the accumulator, with pre- and post-push sub-call witnesses bracketing the push. Relies on R591's indexed sub-call witness from parseFlowSequenceLoop_step_subwit. Axiom set [propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound].)
    • ScalarTokenPinned — Reflection 593 (scalar token content pinning (SS5.12). scanDoubleQuoted_tokens_push_content: when the scanner input is '"' :: escapeString content ++ '"' :: rest in flow context, scanDoubleQuoted pushes exactly .scalar content .doubleQuoted -- content pinned, no existential. Derived by combining the existing exists c push (scanDoubleQuoted_tokens_push) with the lastRealTokenVal? pinning from scanDoubleQuoted_flow_ok and YamlToken.scalar.inj. scanNextToken_flow_scalar_filtered_push_content lifts this to the scanNextToken level. Axiom set [propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound] plus native_decide axioms from collectDoubleQuotedLoop_escapeString_succeeds and its escape-character dependencies.)
    • ScannerSpanLocality — Reflection 596 (scanner-span-locality body-content fact for all-scalar flow sequences (Front-B, SS5.12). emitList_allScalar_body_content_at: for a non-empty all-scalar flow sequence emitList items scanned from a flow-context state, produces a body block with block.length = 2 * items.length - 1 and block[2*j]!.val = .scalar items[j].content .doubleQuoted at each even index. Proof by direct list induction parallel to emitList_scans_safebody in BlockProducers.lean: scanNextToken_flow_scalar_filtered_push_content pins the scalar head token; scanNextToken_flow_comma_filtered_push + scanNextToken_preprocess_flow_ws1 absorb the ", " separator; the IH provides the tail block and the indexing hops use List.getElem!_cons_succ twice to skip the two prefix tokens. Associativity fix: rest_1 := ',' :: ... named as a let binding so that h_chars ▸ hcorr produces hcorr_h : ScannerSurfCorr s (esc ++ rest_1) with rest_1 as an opaque parameter -- avoids the ++ left-vs-right associativity mismatch that rw at hcorr would leave. Axiom set: R593's set (propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound, plus native_decide axioms from double-quoted scanner and escape functions).)
    • CasesReordersTelescope — Reflection 611 (rename_i x₁ … xₙ renames the last n inaccessible hypotheses in CONTEXT order, and cases does NOT introduce a constructor's fields in DECLARATION order — it floats binders that nothing depends on ahead of the ones that do. A rename_i list written by reading the constructor therefore binds the wrong hypotheses, and the failure surfaces downstream as an "application type mismatch" against a SIBLING field's type, never as a naming complaint at the rename_i itself. Toy Frame.held (a b c d) (h : Entries a b) (hcl : Closeable h) (hcomma : Comma b c) (hsep : Sep c d) models L4YAML's SeqFrame.betweenHeld: hcl is DEPENDENT (it mentions the proof h, not just its indices), hcomma is position-only, and cases floats hcomma ahead of h while the h/hcl dependency block moves as a unit — so declared hentries, hcl, hcomma, hsep becomes context hcomma, hentries, hcl, hsep. order_probe is the METHOD: a trace_state in the arm whose output is PINNED by #guard_msgs, so a future reshuffle fails this file rather than some consumer's rename_i. Three arms on one goal: via_cases_with (STABLE — cases h with | held … binds by constructor field order, immune to the permutation, preferred for arms you expect to edit), via_rename_i_context_order (correct list, from the probe), and rename_i_declaration_order_binds_swapped (THE TRAP — the declaration-order list still elaborates, but hcl is what carries Entries a b, so the proof term that type-checks reads as nonsense). same_names_different_order + dependency_block_stays_adjacent (by decide) make the permutation a decidable fact: the two orders are permutations, so COUNTING fields never detects it and no arity check fires; index-determined fields (a, d, the Tail) are substituted away, so the 8 declared binders yield 6 inaccessibles. Diagnose by tracing, never by reading the constructor. All three arms axiom-FREE)
    • DoEarlyExitDuplicatesContinuation — Reflection 613 (a statement-style early-exit guard if cond then throw e inside a do block does NOT elaborate to an ite over the rest of the block: Lean wraps the continuation in a JOIN POINT, have __do_jp := fun __r => <rest>, and branches to it from both sides. One guard, one layer; a SECOND consecutive guard nests a second layer AROUND the first. That nesting is what proofs are coupled to and it is invisible in the source. Three consequences, each demonstrated: (1) split at h on the two-layer shape descends into the join point and splits the INNER guard first, leaving h textually unchanged — it does not error, it silently produces goals the script did not anticipate; (2) simp normalizing the hypothesis zeta-expands a layer and inlines <rest> at BOTH branch references, so k layers give 2^k copies — with a real dispatcher arm that is how split's internal simp hits maximum number of steps exceeded, a SIZE failure distinct from the OPACITY failure the same construct causes when rw [if_pos]/split cannot see the arm at all (DOCS item 9a); (3) the fix is NOT "convert to an else chain" — that re-shapes the arm so downstream scripts break too — but to add ZERO new guards, folding the new rejection into the existing one via a helper returning Option Err checked with if let some e := … then throw e. A baseline arm (oneGuard), the THREE ways to add a second rejection to it (twoGuards, elseDo, merged, proved behaviourally identical by a #guard sweep so the choice is visibly only about shape) and a growth probe (threeGuards, a genuinely different function, present only to show the layers nesting one per guard). invert_merged is invert_one's script VERBATIM; invert_two needs a by_cases enumeration instead. Count the ifs in the arm before adding one — that count, not the guard's logic, is the interface. DOUBLES AS A TOOLCHAIN CANARY: SS4 pins the elaborated term of every shape with #guard_msgs in #print, and SS3 pins the tactic behaviour with fail_if_success, so a Lean bump that moves do-desugaring or split's reach into join points fails HERE rather than as diffuse breakage across the scanner-inversion proofs. Axioms [propext]. L4YAML DOCS item 9c, 2026-08-07: the indexed block-scalar arm already had two guards, a third blew the step limit in Proofs/Scanner/IndexedDispatch.lean, the else do rewrite moved the failure rather than removing it, and folding 9c's check into the existing header check as blockScalarPreErrIx needed ZERO proof edits in that file)

    Porting, modularization & increment economics #

    Splitting, modularization & increment sizing (Reflections 352) #

    • ReadAheadConsumerProofState — Reflection 352 (read-ahead the CONSUMER chain to de-alarm a terse downstream step-list: proof-STATE, not prose, sets the pace. A trajectory de-risk that read steps (3)–(6) below the two FlowSubrangesOk tokens := sorry sites BEFORE grinding the mechanical locator bricks. A dependency map lists downstream steps as one-liners ("drive X … thread Y … wire Z"); a one-liner is INDISTINGUISHABLE between already-proven / trivial-plumbing / a whole parallel-effort — only the source's theorem … := by vs := sorry tells them apart (plan_prose_is_state_blind: the plan exposes one constant bit across all states). A first-pass SURVEY flagged EmitScansInFlowRecEntry "definition only, no proof"; FALSE — emit_scans_in_flow_rec_entry (NonemptyStructure.lean:2418) proves it from Grammable. The survey checked the def, not the PRODUCER (survey_overalarms_vs_source, source_verdict_reads_producer_not_def: read producer not def). Net: no hidden deep obligation in (3)–(6); per-window facts carrier-supplied inside the proven seqWindowRecSeqBody, map RecMapBody step-lemmas landed, single open funnel = seqRoot_seqInteriorSeparators's desc (which the nestedSeq locator builds). Verdict — not circling: the tree is bounded and SHALLOWER than the terse map read. THE PAYOFF is a PACING decision (no_hidden_wall): no wall behind the mechanical bricks ⇒ BATCH them instead of one-per-session. Grep for a PRODUCER (: T := by/→ T/_of_), not the def T; treat a multi-agent survey as a LEAD, source as the verdict. Complements [[ref-probe-deferred-universal-before-producing]] (probes a PRODUCER target; this probes a CONSUMER chain before pacing) and pairs with [[ref-leaf-production-is-prefix-slice]] (the bricks this cleared the runway for))

    • AlternativeRoutesRideTheFrame — Reflection 680 (when a construct's ROLE is decided after it is built, the two ends know different halves: the OPEN knows every frame the enclosing construct offers and not which one the thing will fill, the CLOSE knows the characters and has lost the frame — its route ends in the FINISHED object, and a closure whose codomain has no occurrence of its argument cannot be run backwards. So carry the alternative routes on the frame as optional fields and let the close spend the one the next token names (paid_answers_both vs valueOnly_punts_key, the punt decided by rfl on data because an equation between PROOFS is free); what the frame owes is a route and a HEAD BUILDER, since the head is a function of the content only the close has, and each half alone is worth nothing (route_alone_insufficient/head_alone_insufficient, no_uniform_key_route: what the frame has does not determine the missing route). Bundle the routes in ONE field and the forwarding sites never move.     • SplitTheFactsNotTheQuestion — Reflection 681 (when two situations reach the SAME question with different facts, split the FACTS and ask the question once; splitting the question — a branch per situation, each with its own copy of the answers — costs coverage silently, because the branch written second gets whichever answers its author reached for, and every answer missing there reads afterwards as its own deferral CLASS (asked_once_landed vs duplicate_loses; loss_is_per_answer: the loss counts one per answer, not one per missing fact). State each fact at the weakest form BOTH sides supply and make optional the one only the first can give — a fact one side lacks is a reason to weaken that conjunct, not to duplicate the question.     Folds, packaging & reduction by import (Reflections 225, 227, 257, 491–497) #

    • ReductionByImport — Reflection 225 (reduction by import — retype, not shrink)
    • ConvergentReduction — Reflection 227 (convergence corollary of reduction-by-import)
    • FoldConsumerChain — Reflection 257 (fold a joint-by-joint consumer chain into ONE lemma keyed only on the producer's contract, before writing the producer — the fold costs nothing and cannot regress but converts a scattered residual into one typed boundary: jBound (boundary-anchoring, recovers 2 ≤ lo from the frame token toks[0]! = SS) and jPack (universal packaging) compose into targetOfProducer, keyed only on the abstract producer hProd and the in-scope frame fact hSS; located_via_fold shows an arbitrary-window Located then follows from the producer + frame alone; positive prod_good : Producer good 2 3 := by decide on good = [SS,OB,A,CB,SE], negative neg_no_ss on bad = [OB,A,CB] where the opener guard holds at lo = 1 yet 2 ≤ 1 is false — the frame token is load-bearing for the fold)
    • RescopeAssembleCostIsGuardReadSites — Reflection 491 (the TERMINAL ASSEMBLE of a guard re-thread is GREP-BOUNDED: its cost is exactly the guard hypothesis's READ SITES, each swapped to a pre-landed child twin, while the locate/floor/close SKELETON is guard-AGNOSTIC and copies verbatim. Step γ′ landed seqEnclosingLocate_of_seqOpener_nested_seq, the _seq re-thread of the R485 enclosingLocate assemble keyed on the root-TRUE weaker guard FlowBodyContentDeepSeq ([[RootSeedNeedsRootTrueGuard]] R488, [[RethreadStaysInWeakerTwinFamily]] R489). A guard hypothesis h_deep funnels to a SMALL enumerable set of read sites — the LEAF constructors building the deliverable's guard-typed conjuncts; EVERYTHING ELSE (locate the matching close, the child floor, the close < hi re-derivation, the guard-NEUTRAL window constructor) reads OTHER hypotheses (window facts, locator) and is GUARD-AGNOSTIC. So cost(re-scope an assembler) = (grep h_deep) read sites, each a SWAP to the corresponding pre-landed child twin, + (swap the guard TYPE in signature + conclusion); the bulk skeleton copies verbatim. Here h_deep funnelled to EXACTLY two sites — site 1 swap flowBodyContentDeep_child_bracket ↦ flowBodyContentDeepSeq_child_bracket (R489, passing the located typed opener for the weaker delta) and site 2 swap flowBodyContent_child_bracket ↦ flowBodyContent_child_bracket_seq (R490, which also DROPS the close argument) — the ~50-line locate/floor/close block byte-identical to R485. CONSUMES R489 + R490 (both _seq child twins retype into consumed ones, [[ReductionByImport]]) and re-uses R485's skeleton unchanged ([[RootSeedRecursiveProducerSwap]]: the slice/window framing is guard-agnostic, only the body producer swaps). Landed seqEnclosingLocate_of_seqOpener_nested_seq (axioms [propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound] — IDENTICAL to its strong R485 parent, the Classical.choice inherited from the shared locator skeleton, no sorryAx, frontier still 4). Transferable rule: when a guard re-thread reaches its terminal ASSEMBLE, don't re-prove it — GREP the guard hypothesis in the strong parent's body; its read sites are the only edits, each swapping to a child twin built in a PRIOR turn, the guard type swapping in signature/conclusion. This is WHY the child twins are built first one per turn: they convert the parent assemble from a proof obligation into a mechanical grep-bounded copy — the assemble is the last brick AND the cheapest. The structural-GUARD analog of [[DepthHypCostIsItsEqualityReads]] (a scalar-baseline hypothesis, where each read either swaps to a depth-general twin or re-derives one equality) — here the hypothesis is a whole inductive GUARD and every read swaps uniformly to its _seq twin, no equality re-derivation. Toy: window n, Strong/Weak the two guard families, skeleton the guard-agnostic block (byte-identical call in both assemblers), childGuard_strong/childGuard_weak + content_strong/content_weak the pre-landed per-leaf twins, assemble_strong (R485) vs assemble_seq (R491) differing in EXACTLY the two guard-reading calls + the guard type. Fully axiom-free (direct term proofs over Nat ≤, no tactics). Continues [[RethreadStaysInWeakerTwinFamily]] + [[UnifiedResidualRoutesThroughOneInvariant]] (this assembles their child twins); pairs with [[ReductionByImport]], [[RootSeedRecursiveProducerSwap]], [[AdditiveParallelType]], generalizes [[DepthHypCostIsItsEqualityReads]] from scalar baseline to structural guard)
    • TransportingLemmaTwinsZeroBodyEdits — Reflection 492 (a TRANSPORTING lemma — guard OBTAINED-then-rethreaded, never inspected — re-threads to its _seq twin with ZERO body edits: a SIGNATURE-ONLY swap, the FLOOR of the grep-bounded cost. Step γ′ landed seqWidthEnc_of_enclosingLocate_and_recIH_seq, the FlowBodyContentDeepSeq-keyed twin of R475 seqWidthEnc_of_enclosingLocate_and_recIH — the next link of the _seq re-thread UP from the leaf assemble seqEnclosingLocate_of_seqOpener_nested_seq (R491, [[RescopeAssembleCostIsGuardReadSites]]). The TRANSPORT vs CONSTRUCT distinction: R491's leaf assemble CONSTRUCTS the deliverable's guard conjuncts at the child-bracket constructors, so its guard hypothesis is READ at each leaf and the twin cost EXACTLY those read sites (2 body swaps); R475 never constructs the guard — it obtains h_deep : FlowBodyContentDeep from its enclosingLocate hypothesis, refines it straight into the conclusion's matching slot, threads the conclusion's IH premise straight into recIH, and never matches/destructures/inspects it. Grep the guard in R475's BODY: ZERO hits — every occurrence is in the SIGNATURE (4 positions: enclosingLocate deliverable, recIH premise, conclusion deliverable, conclusion IH premise), all transported. So cost(re-scope a TRANSPORTING lemma) = swap the guard TYPE at its signature positions, BODY copies byte-identical (0 edits) — the FLOOR of the grep-bounded cost (read-site count literally 0). This is WHY a whole re-thread chain is cheap: cost concentrates in the few CONSTRUCTING leaves (R489/R490/R491), every PLUMBING link (R475→R492) re-keys for free. Landed seqWidthEnc_of_enclosingLocate_and_recIH_seq (axioms [propext, Quot.sound] — IDENTICAL to R475's strong parent, and NOTABLY no Classical.choice: pure transport via omega never touches the locator skeleton; no sorryAx, frontier still 4). Verified-but-unconsumed until seqWidthEnc_of_recIH_seq (the R486-twin) feeds it. Transferable rule: classify a re-thread link TRANSPORT vs CONSTRUCT before editing — a transporting (plumbing) lemma's twin is signature-only (0 body edits), a constructing (assemble/leaf) lemma's twin costs its grep'd read sites. Toy: window n, Strong/Weak the two guard families, transport_generic {G} the guard-AGNOSTIC core (proof term mentions no guard — the formal content of "byte-identical body"), transport_strong (R475) vs transport_seq (R492) each the IDENTICAL one-liner application of the core differing ONLY in the signature guard, plus a construct_strong/construct_seq contrast whose bodies DO differ at the read site. Fully axiom-free. The structural-GUARD FLOOR of [[RescopeAssembleCostIsGuardReadSites]] (when read sites = 0, even the swaps vanish) and of [[DepthHypCostIsItsEqualityReads]]; continues [[RethreadStaysInWeakerTwinFamily]] + [[RootSeedNeedsRootTrueGuard]], pairs with [[ReductionByImport]], [[AdditiveParallelType]])
    • LiftRekeysByGuardKeyedChildNames — Reflection 493 (a LIFT — a lemma whose body COMPOSES child-lemma calls — re-keys to a guard's weaker twin via one NAME swap per guard-KEYED child it invokes; guard-NEUTRAL calls are free. The swap unit at a lift is the guard-keyed NAME, not the guard READ ([[TransportingLemmaTwinsZeroBodyEdits]] measured the guard READ to twin a lemma's OWN body; a PARENT that CALLS a child names it, and a name is guard-typed). Step γ′ landed seqWidthEnc_of_recIH_seq, the FlowBodyContentDeepSeq-keyed twin of R486 seqWidthEnc_of_recIH — the LIFT above the R492 transporting assemble + R491 constructing leaf. R486 calls THREE lemmas: (1) seqWidthEnc_of_enclosingLocate_and_recIH — a TRANSPORTING child (R475/R492; its body twins for free) but its NAME is guard-keyed ⇒ swap the call to …_seq (R492); (2) seqOpenerType_of_located_and_gate — guard-NEUTRAL (names no content guard) ⇒ UNCHANGED character-for-character; (3) seqEnclosingLocate_of_seqOpener_nested — a CONSTRUCTING child (R485/R491) ⇒ swap the call to …_seq (R491). So the lift's body cost = TWO named swaps (the two guard-keyed children), NOT the "≈1 swap" an earlier read predicted by counting only the constructing call — the transport/construct distinction governs each CHILD's own twinning, it does NOT discount the lift's CALL to a transporting child. cost(re-key a LIFT) = #(guard-KEYED child lemmas it invokes), each a NAME swap to the child's twin; guard-NEUTRAL invocations free. Why "transport ⇒ verbatim" is right for the child, wrong for the parent's call: transport means the child's PROOF TERM is guard-agnostic (one core, byte-identical body), but the parent does not COPY the child's body — it NAMES the child, and the strong name has the strong type, so the call swaps exactly like a constructing child's. Landed seqWidthEnc_of_recIH_seq (axioms [propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound] — IDENTICAL to its strong R486 parent, the Classical.choice inherited from the constructing leaf's locator skeleton, no sorryAx, frontier still 4). Transferable rule: to re-key a lift, COUNT the guard-keyed lemma names in its body — each is one name swap to that child's already-landed twin; guard-neutral helpers and the proof skeleton are free. Toy: window n, Strong/Weak the two guard families, Disc a guard-NEUTRAL discriminator, transport_child_strong/_seq (byte-identical bodies — transport), neutral_helper (no twin — guard-free), construct_child_strong/_seq (bodies differ at the read — construct), lift_strong (R486) vs lift_seq (R493) differing in EXACTLY the two guard-keyed child names + the signature guard, the neutral call verbatim. Fully axiom-free. Sharpens + CORRECTS the R492 prediction of [[TransportingLemmaTwinsZeroBodyEdits]] (the call-site swap unit is the NAME, so a transporting child still costs a name swap at the lift), composes [[RescopeAssembleCostIsGuardReadSites]] (the constructing child) + R492 (the transporting child); pairs with [[ReductionByImport]] (each swapped child retypes from verified to consumed), [[FoldConsumerChainToProducerContract]] (the lift reads off the boundary's whole spec as one signature), [[AdditiveParallelType]])
    • LiftForwardsDroppedDerivationPremise — Reflection 494 (a transporting LIFT's re-key cost is #(guard-keyed child names) PLUS the child twin's DROPPED-DERIVATION premise — the sharpening of [[LiftRekeysByGuardKeyedChildNames]] (R493: cost = #guard-keyed child names). Step γ′ pivoted from the PRODUCER side (h_widthEnc built keyed on FlowBodyContentDeepSeq, R489→R493) to the CONSUMER side: seqLocalCarrier_of_widthEnc (R446) extracts the weaker h_deep : FlowBodyContentDeepSeq and feeds it down seqDescent_provider_of_located → seqChild_safeBodyUnit → recseqentry_seqbracket_oracle. That dropped self-derivation becomes a THREADED premise the lift must FORWARD — h_ne is a new hypothesis of the twin, passed straight to the oracle twin. So cost(re-key a LIFT) = #(guard-keyed child names) + #(child-twin premises the weaker guard FORCED that this lift must forward) [R494: 1 keyed name + 1 forwarded h_ne]. The leaf cannot SOURCE h_ne: the empty seq [] (j = p+1, tokens[p+1]! = ]) satisfies EVERY non-h_ne hypothesis (h_pj, h_jclose, h_inner, h_floor), so it is genuinely un-derivable at the leaf and climbs the chain until an ancestor knows the body is a GENUINE non-empty seq ([[PrefixGateReconstructedFromBoundary]]). Sharpens [[LiftRekeysByGuardKeyedChildNames]] (adds the forwarded-premise term), composes [[TransportingLemmaTwinsZeroBodyEdits]] (the transport body) + [[WindowAbsoluteGateSubsetRestriction]] (the gate that drops the derivation) + [[PrefixGateReconstructedFromBoundary]] (where the premise is finally sourced); continues [[RethreadStaysInWeakerTwinFamily]] + [[RootSeedNeedsRootTrueGuard]], pairs with [[ReductionByImport]], [[AdditiveParallelType]])
    • ClimbingPremiseTerminatesAtCaseSplit — Reflection 495 (the climbing dropped-derivation premise of [[LiftForwardsDroppedDerivationPremise]] (R494) terminates at a CASE SPLIT, not a single discharge — the sharpening surfaced landing seqDescent_provider_of_located_seq, the FlowBodyContentDeepSeq-keyed twin of seqDescent_provider_of_located, the consumer-chain link ABOVE the R494 leaf lift seqChild_safeBodyUnit_seq. R494 predicted this ancestor would SOURCE the forwarded h_ne. It does NOT source it unconditionally: the weaker guard ADMITS the very degenerate input the strong guard's UNCONDITIONAL opener field excluded — an empty enclosing seq [ … [] … ] whose inner empty seq's gated window has j = p+1 and tokens[p+1]! = .flowSequenceEnd ⇒ h_ne is FALSE — and the dispatcher hands the descent provider windows with a ≤ b (NOT a < b), so that degenerate input REACHES this ancestor. So the premise terminates at a CASE SPLIT on the discriminator the strong guard had ENCODED (p + 1 < j, enclosing seq non-empty): the NON-degenerate branch SOURCES h_ne from the interior floor (tokens[p+1]! = .flowSequenceEnd ⇒ balance p (p+2) = 1 + (-1) = 0 contradicts the matched-bracket floor ≥ 1) and routes through the R494 lift; the DEGENERATE branch (j = p+1) sees the gated window collapse to a = b = p+1, both separator facts VACUOUS, so produces the existential ⟨p+1, p+1, …⟩ DIRECTLY (balance loS a = 0 is h_body_bal verbatim), BYPASSING the leaf it could not feed. The ENTIRE preamble is character-identical to the strong parent ([[TransportingLemmaTwinsZeroBodyEdits]] — reads no deep field), so cost(terminating ancestor) = #(guard-keyed child names) + (source the premise on the re-discovered discriminator) + (produce the deliverable vacuously on the degenerate branch the weaker guard admits). Landed seqDescent_provider_of_located_seq (axioms [propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound] — IDENTICAL to its strong parent, no sorryAx, frontier still 4). Toy: WeakG (gated, holds at the degenerate WeakG 0), oracle_seq/lift_seq (forward h_ne, R494), discriminator D n := 2 ≤ n with ne_of_D : D n → NE n, deliverable Goal n := ∀ k, k+1 < n → Packaged n (VACUOUS for n < 2), and the brick terminate case-splitting on D — non-degenerate routes through lift_seq (sourcing h_ne from D), degenerate produces Goal directly; degenerate_reaches_ancestor : WeakG 0 ∧ ¬ NE 0 re-exhibits the un-sourceability and example : Goal 0 := terminate 0 ⟨id⟩ lands via the bypass.     • WiderClosureArgumentLosesInvariant — Reflection 621 (a CLOSURE ARGUMENT wider than the values that actually flow through it is not spare generality, it is a DISCARDED INVARIANT — and the diagnostic is in the callers: every one of them applies the same injection to reach the type. A nested flow frame does not store its parent; it carries inject, a closure folding this frame's eventual result into it, and its argument was SFlowNode — [161] ns-flow-node, the widest thing a flow position can hold. Widest looks safe. It was why ONE case could not be written at all: [&a [b]] holds a [96] c-ns-properties run when a nested collection opens, so the properties must WRAP the child's node (SFlowNode.propsContent), and propsContent takes [158] ns-flow-content — because properties may not decorate an alias, [104] c-ns-alias-node being an ALTERNATIVE to the properties-bearing form of [161], never its content. A closure promising only "a node" cannot be wrapped. §2 alias_has_no_decorated_preimage (axiom-FREE) and wide_wrap_cannot_round_trip are that gap: every decorated thing erases to .content, so alias has no preimage and the wide argument admits a value the target has no room for. §3 shows the narrowing costs nothing where it is used — narrow_agrees_on_content is rfl. §4 is the diagnostic, computed rather than recalled: model each caller by the value it really has and check the constructor table — wideArgs.all (· != alias) says the alias was in the argument's RANGE and never in its IMAGE, and every call site was already writing SFlowNode.content (…) by hand, so narrowing MOVES one wrapper from caller to callee rather than adding one. §5 is why the mistake is easy: widening a closure's argument LOOKS like weakening a hypothesis, but the position is contravariant — it strengthens the obligation on whoever SUPPLIES the closure, and the supplier is the one holding the invariant; both widen and narrow are total (widen_narrow_id), so nothing fails to typecheck, the new consumer simply cannot be written, and widen_invents_an_answer names the information lost.     • StoredVsProjectedRecursionEdge — Reflections 251 + 252 (a recursive-deliverable inductive's STORAGE choices set the PRODUCER's recursion graph — Entry.seq stores the recursive Body interior so the producer's seq case recurses (buildEntry → buildBody), while Entry.map projects only the flat bal interior = 0 so the producer's map case is a LEAF (buildEntry → bal_emitList, never buildBody), severing the recursion edge; witnessed by mapLeaf (a flat leaf builder for the projecting member, with no seqLeaf counterpart) and by built_map_empty : Entry [om,cm] (the projecting member accepts an interior with NO Body, even though not_body_nil shows Body [] is uninhabited and the storing member therefore needs a dedicated seqEmpty); the producer targeting only projecting members is strictly smaller and dependency-light — a single self-contained induction — and in a producer family the projection severs the cross-producer edge, ordering the family.     • NearLeafMirrorShedsMachinery — Reflection 293 (a near-leaf mirror SHEDS the recursion machinery its recursive sibling needed — the toy of recseqentry_mapbracket_oracle, the { … } mirror of the recursive seq oracle. RBody is the recursive deliverable (inhabited only by its constructors, UNINHABITED on []), WB l := pbal l = 0 ∧ ∀ i, pbal (l.take i) ≥ 0 the near-leaf (flat prefix-balance, inhabited on []). NEGATIVE: not_rbody_empty : ¬ RBody [] shows the empty case is a HOLE for the recursive sibling (so the seq oracle's non-emptiness guard is real), FREE for the near-leaf (WB [] holds) — the storage asymmetry's two opposite signs on one input; not_wb_opn : ¬ WB [opn] confirms WB is not vacuous, the near-leaf oracle being IH-free, not content-free. The lesson: write the recursive sibling first (it forces the full machinery), then mirror the near-leaf by DELETING the IH and the descend/non-emptiness guard, not adding to them)
    • UnifiedResidualForcesSiblingProjection — Reflection 499 (a unifying consumer forces a second, fuller projection of the producer's recursive deliverable that the split consumer never needed — the number of projections a producer must supply is set by the CONSUMER's choice to split-vs-unify its residual, not by the deliverable's own shape.     • CoercionDissolvesForcedProjection — Reflection 500 (a forced second projection — R499's prediction that a unifying consumer forces a fuller projection of the producer's deliverable — DISSOLVES when the weaker projection is COERCIBLE to the fuller one. Building the _seq FlowBodyContent descend edge flowBodyContent_descend_seq (R500) refuted R499 for this instance: the unit projection (R494 seqChild_safeBodyUnit_seq) ALONE serves the unified h_feContent of flowBodyContent_of_deepSeq. The unified residual splits INTERIOR (k+1<j) ∪ BOUNDARY (k+1=j); BOTH come from the ONE windowed SafeBodyUnit via the EXISTING seqEnclosingFacts_of_windowed_safebodyunit — interior through seqInteriorFeContentStart_of_windowed_safebodyunit, which already COERCES SafeBodyUnit → SafeBody internally (SafeBodyUnit_safeBody ContentStartTok_ne_flowEntry) before calling SafeBody_array_flowEntry_window, and boundary through noTrailingSepFact VACUOUSLY. So the "fuller projection" R499 thought forced is recovered from the unit projection by a head-conditioned COERCION already packaged in the consumer; seqChild_safeBody_seq (R499) stays a valid verified-but-unconsumed sibling, off the critical path. The number of DISTINCT projections a producer must supply is reduced by any head/element-conditioned coercion Weak → Strong on the consume side: the unified residual routes through (weak projection) + (coercion), not a second projection. Two conditions make the coercion possible — and they are exactly what R499's minimal-pair demo dropped: the WEAK projection must carry the INTERIOR data (R499's unitProj := head? carried none ⇒ genuinely non-coercible, the regime where the forcing holds), and a HEAD condition must close the one head-gap. flowBodyContent_descend_seq also FORWARDS h_ne (the empty-seq guard the weak openerContentStart can't self-derive) and assembles via the UNIFIED flowBodyContent_of_deepSeq; axioms [propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound] identical to its strong twin, frontier still 4. Toy: Deliv (nonempty all-true list) models RecSeqBody; Weak (proper-TAIL all-true + content-typed head, ≠ false) models SafeBodyUnit; Strong := ∀ x ∈ l, x = true models SafeBody; Deliv.toWeak is R494, Weak.toStrong is the SafeBodyUnit_safeBody coercion, strong_via_coercion := h.toWeak.toStrong is the DISSOLUTION (Strong with no direct Deliv → Strong); head_condition_load_bearing : (∀ x ∈ [false].tail, x = true) ∧ ¬ Strong [false] witnesses the head condition is genuinely consumed — the interior alone does not close the gap. Sharpens/partially-CORRECTS [[UnifiedResidualForcesSiblingProjection]] (the forcing is conditional on non-coercibility); composes [[CompleteProjectionFamilyForNewMember]], [[ReductionByImport]] (the dissolution reuses an existing consumer helper rather than a new projection))
    • InlinePayloadToIndex — Reflection 610 (a state type that packages a Prop-valued payload cannot be indexed by "which payload constructor built it" — the payload is proof-irrelevant, so nothing can read the constructor back out and no index can be attached to the wrapper after the fact; when an OUTSIDE guard distinguishes exactly those constructors, the payload must be spread into the outer type, one outer constructor per payload shape, each naming its own index. Toy: Part (three shapes of a partially-built flow collection — empty [, items a finished entry, held entry-plus-,) under two wrappers: Wrapped (packages Part, one constructor) vs Inlined (the same three shapes spread out, each indexed by Tail = .sep/.value). inlined_forget + wrapped_conflates + inlined_separates show the two carry the SAME inhabitants and differ only in what the type records. CRUX pair: hold_inlined (the , transition Inlined .value l → Inlined .sep (l ++ [comma]), provable because cases never reaches the two degenerate shapes — their index is .sep) vs hold_wrapped_false (the same transition is outright FALSE on the wrapper: Wrapped [] holds but [comma] is not a Part, the leading-comma [, rejection) plus hold_wrapped_false_held (the ,, shape). The coupling half: guardOk/tailOfList model the scanner-side check and the index reading, and guard_matches_tail_shapes (by decide) asserts they agree on every shape — the index is useless unless pinned to what the guard actually reads.     • MirrorReadsConjunctNotProjection — Reflection 464 (a flat→strengthened producer MIRROR is a pure TEXT-SWAP iff the flat proof reads each per-item fact from the predicate's STANDALONE CONJUNCT, not from a PROJECTION off the per-item inductive — the toy of STEP D step 8's deep body assemblers.     • ProjectAtTheConsumer — Reflection 662 (a producer that has decided a SET must hand over the SET: the moment it projects onto the one question its first consumer asks, everything else it knew is gone, and the loss is invisible because the projected fact is still true, still used, and still named after the thing it proves — the next consumer prices a DISCARDED fact as a missing derivation. Carrying the set costs the first consumer one mono, which is the step the producer was taking anyway. And an escape is a projection: a shared consumer's fallback written as the CONCLUSION it produces (h : Goal) has thrown away the occasion, so every caller must pay it and none can refuse it; written as h : Residue → Goal the paying callers are unchanged (fun _ => h) and a caller that can empty the premise pays nofun — the case distinction moves to the call sites, where it already existed, and the shared lemma is NOT split. Goal is Residue → Goal projected at the producer, so the two halves are one rule. Toy: suffixOK (the validator's own allowlist, 2 of 5 heads) vs notOpen (item 10's stored projection, 4 of 5); projection_is_sound shows why nothing flags it and projected_fact_cannot_answer (notOpen .dash = true ∧ suffixOK .dash = false) is the cost; Line P indexes the predicate by its stop set, Line.mono is the projection at the consumer, old_consumer_still_served is one mono longer and new_consumer_now_served is immediate. Escape half: Escape₁ = Goal (bare_escape_is_the_conclusion) vs Escape₂ r = r → Goal, with old_payer := fun _ => g, refusing_payer := fun h => Bool.noConfusion h, and escape_from_refutation_alone — v2 inhabited from the refutation with no Goal in hand, which v1 cannot express; paying_caller_unchanged + refusing_caller_has_no_argument show both feed one body. Composes [[StoredVsProjectedRecursionEdge]] (which fields a type stores sets its producer's shape; this is which STRENGTH a fact stores setting its consumers' reach) + [[ProjectionFamilyCompletion]]; the escape half is the dual of [[ResidueWasAStateNeverEntered]] — that item gave a case the field it needed to name its state, this one gives the ESCAPE the premise it needs to be refused)

    • EscapeNarrowsToItsResidue — Reflection 663 (when the fact refutes only PART of an escape's residue, the escape survives and the site survives — and the move is to re-type the escape by the premise that SURVIVED: Residue c → Goal becomes Residue ':' → Goal, the refuted members are discharged at the call sites where the fact lives, the paying callers are unchanged, and the type now NAMES what is left, so the remaining work is held by the type-checker instead of by a sentence in a document. The premise is forced, not chosen — only_the_survivor_inhabits reads the surviving member off the residue by inversion, which a conclusion-typed escape cannot do because Goal mentions no member. And two producers can decide one set for opposite reasons: a validator REFUSES the character (read it, errored), a walk ABSORBS it (never parked in front of one) — refusal_and_absorption_agree is a theorem, not a definition, which is what lets ONE field carry both families. Toy: nodeStop (§7.5's tail at the three block indicators, 1 of 3), Wide := ∀ i, Escape (Residue i) vs Narrow := Escape (Residue .colon) with narrow_of_wide free and wide_of_narrow spending the refutation at the two dead members; the_ledger_moved (owed 3 → 1) beside the_site_survives (6 = 6), which is the honest reading of a partial refutation. Continues [[ProjectAtTheConsumer]] — that item gave the escape a premise, this one shows what to do when the premise is only partly empty)

    • CarryTheRouteNotTheCoordinates — Reflection 664 (a carried datum written as the COORDINATES one producer happens to have admits exactly that producer; written as the CONCLUSION they would all reach with them — a function into the goal — it admits every producer that can reach the goal, and the CONSUMER gets shorter, because the coordinates are spent once at the producer that has them. Not the opposite of [[ProjectAtTheConsumer]]: that rule forbids weakening a fact every producer could supply; this one forbids carrying a fact the consumer never asks about. The discriminator is whether EVERY producer can supply the strong form — if the strong form names a construct only one producer has, it is not a stronger version of what the consumer needs, it is one route to it, and carrying it is what keeps the second producer out. Test: state the datum without naming a construct outside the consumer's vocabulary — a stop set survives (662), 'a column-0 line start' does not. Toy: Coords (a landing + a width) vs Compact (the enclosing closure + a width) reaching one Route; the_pack_widened (¬ PackCoords .compact — no landing to name, not an unproved one), route_forgets (two coordinate sets, one route), conversion_is_one_way, and the_work_moved_it_did_not_vanish (frame steps 4 = 4) beside the_consumer_stopped_naming_the_frame (4 → 0); the_head_did_not_move — [193]/[194] take no indent, so the four key-head arms are anchor-free and the entanglement was in the writing.     Guards, gates & threading #

    Threading & re-scoping (Reflections 287, 289–291, 294, 295, 322, 343, 344, 367, 469, 488–490, 501, 521, 533, 624, 626, 660, 672, 683) #

    • GuardThreadingSkeleton — Reflection 287 (the per-window step has a guard-threading skeleton below its grammar core: Reflection 286's combinator left its guard G abstract, and driving it requires G made concrete and shown preserved across each recursion edge before the head-shape grammar can run — that layer is grammar-free and, because a well-chosen G names no collection-specific deliverable type, SHARED across both axes. BodyWin (toy of the real FlowBodyWindow) bundles frame bounds + non-emptiness lo < hi + balanced/Dyck + a content predicate wt, naming NO deliverable type; bodyWin_advance (toy of flowBodyWindow_advance, its proof the real one verbatim) transports the WHOLE guard from [lo, hi) to the tail [m+1, hi) given only the separator's three transport certificates — tail balanced, depths re-base to the outer origin (toy of advanceTail_invariant), content carries to the subrange (toy of WellTyped_subrange) — with the only non-trivial field, Dyck, closed by re-basing each tail prefix to lo then applying the outer Dyck; the non-emptiness premise m+1 < hi (no trailing separator) is DEFERRED to the caller (the head-shape grammar's burden). One guard, both axes: feedSeq/feedMap (stand-ins for producing a RecSeqBody/RecMapBody) are gated by the SAME BodyWin. Positives win_2_6 (a concrete guard) and win_4_6 (bodyWin_advance firing to the tail), #guard feedSeq/feedMap win_2_6 == 4; negatives not_bodyWin_empty (the lo < hi field is LOAD-BEARING — the R285 echo — so the guard EXCLUDES the empty window the deliverable cannot inhabit) and #guard (6 - (3+1)) < (6 - 2) (the ADVANCE edge strictly NARROWS the window, so the combinator's width metric decreases))

    • AdditiveParallelGuard — Reflection 289 (a too-weak shared guard's missing content is its own additive parallel guard — the guard-layer instance of [[ref-additive-parallel-type-over-shared-edit]]. The head-shape dispatch needs "head is content-start", and that is NOT derivable from the bracket guard FlowBodyWindow (balance/dyck/WellTyped): a separator-headed window is still balanced+dyck+WellTyped. BracketWin f lo hi := bal f lo hi = 0 ∧ ∀ i, bal f lo i ≥ 0 toys the bracket guard; dyck_excludes_closer shows the dyck floor genuinely forbids a leading CLOSER (tokDelta (hd lo) ≥ 0), but separator_head_passes_dyck (0 ≥ 0) + separator_head_not_contentstart show a separator head clears the SAME floor while not being content-start, and bracketwin_sep_head/sep_head_not_cs exhibit a whole window passing the bracket guard whose head is a separator — so content-start is not a function of the guard. The fix: name the missing content as a parallel guard ContentWin f hd lo hi := isContentStart (hd lo) ∧ ∀ k, … hd k = .sep → bal f lo k = 0 → isContentStart (hd (k+1)) (toy of FlowBodyContent's headContentStart + feContentStart), defined as what the consumer already eats. POSITIVE: content_advance (toy of flowBodyContent_advance) transports ContentWin to the tail [m+1, hi) by pure re-basing — the tail head re-seated from feContentStart at the separator; content_window_good/content_advance_good fire it on scal sep scal, re-seating hdGood 0 = scal to hdGood 2 = scal across the separator at 1 (#guarded). NEGATIVE: not_contentwin_bad — the empty-entry body scal sep sep scal is NOT a ContentWin (the separator at 1 is followed by another separator, not a content-start head), so feContentStart is the load-bearing field whose failure would break ADVANCE re-seating; the recursive DESCEND content edge — interior heads a nesting level down — is left as the isolated residual)

    • DescendStrengthInvariant — Reflection 290 (a guard sized to its easy (ADVANCE) edge can be structurally incapable of its hard (DESCEND) edge — the sequel to R289 and a fresh instance of [[ref-converse-forward-invariant-asymmetry]] fused with [[ref-probe-deferred-universal-before-producing]]. R289's content guard landed its ADVANCE edge cheaply, and that ease MASKED that the guard cannot carry the DESCEND edge: descending into a nested bracket [k .. j] must hand the interior head hd (k+1) as content-start, but that head sits at balance 1 while every field of the depth-0 guard is gated by bal lo k = 0 — no outer field even mentions the interior head. The edge is not hard to prove; the guard is too weak to carry. Shallow f hd lo hi := isContentStart (hd lo) ∧ ∀ k, … hd k = .sep → bal f lo k = 0 → isContentStart (hd (k+1)) toys R289's FlowBodyContent (depth-0, no opener field); Deep hd lo hi := isContentStart (hd lo) ∧ (∀ k, … tokDelta (hd k) = 1 → isContentStart (hd (k+1))) ∧ (∀ k, … hd k = .sep → isContentStart (hd (k+1))) toys FlowBodyContentDeep — content at ALL depths, balance-FREE. POSITIVE: deep_descend (toy of flowBodyContentDeep_descend) transports Deep into the interior [k+1, j) as a pure RESTRICTION — child head off the parent's opener field at k, child fields the parent's quantifiers restricted, no re-basing/no bracket arithmetic; deep_good/deep_descend_good fire it on the nested [ a a ], yielding the interior guard on [1, 3) with head scal. NEGATIVE: on the leading-separator interior [ , a ] (hdBad = opn sep scal cls), shallow_holds shows the shallow guard HOLDS (its only separator sits at balance 1, so its depth-0 gate bal 0 1 = 0 is vacuous), yet interior_head_not_contentstart shows the interior head hdBad 1 = sep is NOT content-start — so the shallow guard, though true, gives nothing toward the interior guard; not_deep confirms the DEEP guard correctly REJECTS the same stream (its opener field at 0 demands exactly the interior head be content-start). The lesson: probe the hard edge's provability against the guard AS DEFINED — the remedy is a stronger invariant (lift to the descend edge's depth), not more proof effort)

    • LiftOnceMirrorEasyEdge — Reflection 291 (lift once, then MIRROR the easy edge — the dividend-collection sequel to [[ref-easy-edge-guard-fails-hard-edge]] / R290 and the resolution step of [[ref-converse-forward-invariant-asymmetry]]. R290 diagnosed that a guard sized to its easy (ADVANCE) edge can be structurally incapable of its hard (DESCEND) edge, and lifted the content guard to the all-depth, balance-FREE Deep; R291 is the dividend: once lifted, the easy edge is ALREADY PAID FOR. A balance-free guard is a pure RESTRICTION of itself on every sub-window, and that property is direction-agnostic, so the advance edge restricts the same all-depth quantifiers to the tail [m+1, hi) exactly as the descend edge restricts them to the interior [k+1, j) — the ONLY difference is which field re-seats the child head (OPENER at k for descend, SEPARATOR at m for advance). POSITIVE: deep_descend (toy of flowBodyContentDeep_descend) and deep_advance (toy of flowBodyContentDeep_advance) have the IDENTICAL proof skeleton (obtain ⟨_, h_op, h_fe⟩; refine ⟨?_, ?_, ?_⟩; head off one field; the two quantifier fields the parent's restricted by omega), differing only in the one head-seating line (h_op k … vs h_fe m …) — and deep_advance takes NO balance hypothesis and NO size side condition; deep_descend_good fires descend on [ a a ] → [1,3), deep_adv/deep_advance_good fire advance on a , a → [2,3), re-seating hdAdv 2 = scal with zero arithmetic (#guarded). NEGATIVE: re-deriving advance from the depth-0 PROJECTION Shallow costs the balance gate — on [ , , ] (hdNeg = opn sep sep cls, separators at depth 1), shallow_holds_neg shows the shallow guard HOLDS vacuously (its gate bal 0 k = 0 is false at the depth-1 separators), yet interior_after_sep_not_content shows the post-separator head hdNeg 2 = sep is NOT content-start, so the shallow guard gives nothing toward advancing there; not_deep_neg confirms the deep guard's UN-gated separator field is exactly the repair (it rejects the stream). The lesson: after lifting a guard to carry its hard edge, do NOT re-derive the easy edge with the old projection-level machinery — write it as the hard edge's mirror, swapping only the head-seating field)

    • ThreadedGuardPrunesCaseTree — Reflection 294 (a threaded guard PRUNES the case tree — the toy of recseqentry_window_dispatch, the per-window head-shape dispatch the dependency map wrote as a FOUR-way split that is actually THREE. The dispatch reads the head off isContentStart (THREE shapes — scalar / sopen / mopen, never the closer); the deep guard's OpenerContentStart (after a sopen, the next token is content-start) makes the empty-bracket leaf [ ] unreachable, so it is EXCLUDED, not handled — folded into the nested-[ … ] branch. POSITIVE: dispatch is TOTAL over the three content-start heads (its sclose case vacuous, isContentStart sclose = False via h.elim); empty_excluded shows under the guard a sopen-headed window never has sclose next. NEGATIVE: not_content_start_sclose : ¬ isContentStart sclose (the closer is no head); empty_reachable_without_guard shows the empty bracket IS a genuine fourth shape absent the guard — so it is the GUARD, not the deliverable's type, that prunes the branch (the Shape type still carries emptyBracket distinct from nestedSeq). The lesson: a threaded recursion guard is not only a source of premises — a guard strengthened for one purpose can be strong enough to DELETE a branch the deliverable still anticipates, so the structural moves completing a recursion can be fewer than the constructor count)

    • IncompleteProjectionStillFactors — Reflection 295 (an INCOMPLETE guard-projection still factors — the toy of flowBodyContent_of_deep, the assembler that PRODUCES the head-dispatch's FlowBodyContent from the threaded deep guard. When a strengthened carrier projects MOST of a target structure but not all, build the ASSEMBLER that projects the covered part and NAMES the residual, not a heavier carrier. The projection is partial in TWO grains: head is a WHOLE-FIELD free projection (DeepGuard.head and Content.head are the SAME proposition), sepNext is a CASE-SPLIT projection — the carrier covers its INTERIOR (k+1 < n, the sepNextInterior field gated k+1 < n) but not its BOUNDARY (k+1 = n). POSITIVE: assemble builds Content from DeepGuard plus two named residual hypotheses — the head arm a free projection, the sepNext arm rcases-split into projected-interior / residual-boundary. NEGATIVE: valSep_not_derivable — the carrier is SEPARATOR-BLIND, DeepGuard 2 twoScalars holds (two adjacent scalars [a b]) yet valSep (comma-separation, toy of bodySucc) FAILS, so it is genuinely owed; boundary_not_reachable — DeepGuard 2 trailingComma holds ([a ,]) yet sepNext FAILS at the boundary k+1 = n (trailing comma, tok 2 = cls not content-start), so the interior-gated carrier field cannot reach it. The lesson: the residual you isolate — here one whole field (valSep) PLUS one missing CASE of another (sepNext boundary) — is precisely the data the carrier semantically cannot encode; name it, the names are the real owed primitives)

    • ImportForcedParametricGuard — Reflection 322 (thread a recursion guard through an IH-supplying lemma that is UPSTREAM of the guard's definition by making the upstream lemma PARAMETRIC over an abstract predicate Q — the toy of the R322 Q-parametric IH re-versioning across recseqentry_seqbracket_oracle / recseqentry_window_dispatch / seqChild_safeBodyUnit / seqDescent_provider_of_located.     • ContextFreeAdapterKnot — Reflection 343 (a CONTEXT-FREE IH adapter cannot thread a CONTEXT-DEPENDENT carrier fact — the toy behind the (i'-b-B2c-desc-fixpoint-assembly) probe. To break the carrier↔recursion circularity, the carrier-free seqWindowRecSeqBody would thread FlowBodyContent as a recursion G-conjunct instead of looking it up from the ambient carrier. The probe asked: clean ordering or co-recursive knot? KNOT, two ways. Toy: opaque Rec/Content : Nat → Prop (toy RecSeqBody/FlowBodyContent keyed on window width); content_of_rec : ∀ w, Rec w → Content w (the SAME-window edge, toy of SafeBodyUnit-of-RecSeqBody — R296); dispatch : ∀ w, Content w → (∀ w' < w, Rec w') → Rec w (the dispatch needs the CURRENT window's Content + a CONTEXT-FREE Rec-IH, toy recseqentry_window_dispatch). POSITIVE (rec_via_carrier): the GLOBAL carrier ∀ w, Content w closes the producer — it answers Content at every window WITHOUT recursion. NEGATIVE (rec_guarded_needs_carrier + content_same_window): threading Content through the guard buys NOTHING — the context-free IH adapter fun w' hw' => ih w' hw' ? must inject Content w' for ARBITRARY smaller w', and the only carrier-free source content_of_rec needs Rec w' (the output), so the step must take the whole carrier as an undischarged hypothesis (Content w ⟵ Rec w ⟵ dispatch ⟵ Content w, a SAME-window cycle width recursion cannot break; P := Rec ∧ Content does not untie it). RESOLUTION (rec_resolved + content_resolved): the knot cuts only by a Content-FREE dispatch (reconstruct its three head/first-entry facts carrier-free, [[ref-prefix-gate-reconstructed-from-boundary]]); then Rec closes with NO carrier and Content is a downstream COROLLARY. #guards smoke-test the resolved + carrier routes on the trivial model.     • FieldAuditLocalizesKnot — Reflection 344 (a multi-field guard INPUT localizes its dependency knot to ONE load-bearing field — the AUDIT behind the (i'-b-B2c-dispatch-defront) de-front, the move after R343's knot. The pivot removes FlowBodyContent from recseqentry_window_dispatch's input, but the input is a THREE-field guard; the naïve fear is the WHOLE guard creates the cycle. The audit shows otherwise: headContentStart is a FREE projection (definitionally the SAME proposition as FlowBodyContentDeep.headContentStart, which the recursion already carries); feContentStart is DEAD (consumed by NEITHER the dispatch nor either bracket oracle — it serves the advance-tail plumbing, a sibling consumer); bodySucc is the GENUINE residual (R296: no all-depth balance-free form, NOT a field of FlowBodyContentDeep). So the knot localizes to the SINGLE field bodySucc at the SINGLE position the first-entry END. Toy: Deep/Guard structures with shared head target; dispatch_via_guard (carrier route, touches only head+succ). POSITIVE (dispatch_defront + dispatch_via_deep_and_succ): the consumer is inhabitable from the carried Deep (free head) + the lone residual succ, with NO feStart field in its signature; the full-Guard route factors through it.     • OperationalFlagWitnessesHistory — Reflection 624 (before threading a new invariant to carry a HISTORY fact — "no break was crossed", "nothing was emitted", "no indent was popped" — ask whether the machine already RECORDS it. A machine that must ACT on its history has to remember it, in an ordinary field, for its own operational reasons, and that field is a sound witness for free. DOCS had priced this exact fact as unavailable — "a fact about skipToContentLoop that no existing lemma provides", and ScannerSurfCorr cannot supply it because SurfPos carries chars and col and no line — and both halves were true while the conclusion was wrong: consumeNewline sets needIndentCheck := true on every break it consumes, because the NEXT dispatch must re-run the indent check, and nothing inside the loop clears it. §1 proves the only property that matters, monotonicity (step has no arm writing false), and §2 gets the transport from it in four lines: the break branch is REFUTED by the output flag rather than excluded by a hypothesis nobody had, which is why the statement is on the OUTPUT flag and needs no entry condition. §3's sweep over 1093 inputs shows the flag is exactly the expensive oracle ((run start cs).flag == crossed cs, the character walk) from a clear start, over-reports from a raised one on 127 of them, and that the conclusion survives anyway. §4 is the trap, and the reason the search stopped in the wrong place: surf projects (col, remaining) — positions, not history — and noBreak/withBreak agree on it exactly while differing in line, so no lemma over that projection could ever decide the question. A correspondence relation's silence is a property of what it was built to say. Real lemmas: skipToContentLoop_needIndentCheck_mono and skipToContentLoop_line_eq_of_needIndentCheck in Proofs/Scanner/ScannerLinePreservation.lean, sorry-free, with the _preserves_line / _preserves_needIndentCheck families cloned from _preserves_flowStack.     • FlagScopedByTheWrongDimension — Reflection 626 (a flag that bounds a construct's extent can be scoped along the WRONG AXIS — the dual of 624: the machine stored a history, just not the one it must act on. The extent lives on one axis (the ENTRY), the flag records a coordinate on another (the LINE), and the flag is right exactly where the two coincide. Three things keep it alive. §2: on the sublanguage with neither a break nor a separator the two scopes are the SAME FUNCTION (121 of 781 words), which is every example anyone would write down to explain the flag. §3: over the whole corpus both scopes accept 670 words — the same count, different sets — so no summary statistic sees it, only the pointwise difference (28 words). And the failure is a REFUSAL: a flag that SUPPRESSES work makes the machine reject valid input, so nothing wrong is ever emitted and no round-trip or output check fires (625 §4 for why the guard suite cannot either). §4 is the repair's shape — correcting the axis moves 14 words each way, and the two halves are DISJOINT, so fixing the separator boundary says nothing about the break boundary; 12 of the 14 over-suppressed words never cross a break at all, which is why a single-line corpus reaches that half and not the other. Note the tell in run: scoped by the right axis the flag needs NO explicit clearing, because the coordinate comparison expires on its own.     • RefutationOwesTheBranch — Reflection 660 (refuting a runtime check that BRANCHES on recorded state means owning the branch, and no amount of grammar substitutes: you can know the rule, know the check enforces it, and know the input violates it at the cursor, and the check still passes because a record elsewhere sent it to read a clean place (data_alone_admits_a_pass beside no_record_refutes — the conditional is real, not a mirage). What is missing is a fact about the STATE, and it is SMALLER than the one you would reach for: not "no stale record exists", a claim about the whole history, but "the record, if any, is AT the cursor" — at which point both branches read the same place and the conditional is only apparent on the reachable states (branches_agree_at_cursor, refuted_once_the_branch_is_owned, the_minimal_fact_is_weaker). So before pricing a coupling that CONTROLS a branch, ask what the branches do where you land; often they coincide and the invariant you owe is just the one that says you landed there. Where it belongs is the producer, as a REQUIRED field — it is a fact about the scan just performed, not a measurement some producers cannot take ([[OptionalFieldBuysDomain]] the other way round) — and it costs no transport lemma, because the flag that decides the branch is MONOTONE across the step: a break re-arms it, nothing clears it, so monotonicity IS the transport (flag_survives_any_step beside stack_needs_a_hypothesis_about_the_step, monotone_costs_no_transport). That is [[OperationalFlagWitnessesHistory]]'s mechanism doing a second job — 624 reads the flag as a WITNESS of what happened, this reads it as the SELECTOR of what a later check will do. §4 keeps the coordinate straight: the check's earlier branch walks the whole LINE, which is right for an entry that starts its line and blind for a COMPACT opener, and it is not wrong to be ([[GuardInTheWrongCoordinate]]; part_after_an_indicator).     • PremiseTheProducerCanDischarge — Reflection 683 (when a runtime fact holds under EITHER of two conditions, carry it under their DISJUNCTION — not as a hedge, but because that is the only form the induction can transport. Picking the ENTRY condition kills it at the consumer, whose own hypothesis supplies the negation, so the carried implication is vacuous and proves False (entry_alone_is_vacuous_at_the_consumer beside the_fact_holds_there: the fact is true exactly where the form is useless); picking the LANDING condition — "we got here by taking a step" — kills it in the recursion, which cannot see whether the INNER call takes one, and the composition rule itself yields False (landing_alone_does_not_compose). The disjunction composes in one line because the step IS the discharge (either_composes, fun h _ => h (Or.inl rfl), blind to the inner index) and survives the base case (either_at_the_base); the consumer is then TOTAL without either condition (consumer_splits, consumer_is_total), because the branch it cannot discharge is the one a different arm already serves — a walk that crossed no break has its run on the entry line, which is the INLINE case. It is not a compromise: either_implies_entry/either_implies_landing make it the strongest of the three, and entry_does_not_imply_landing/landing_does_not_imply_entry show neither single form was the obvious pick. §7 says why the fact was missing at all — a hop returning only the RUN it walked observes the same thing under a machine that checks and one that does not (run_observation_is_gate_blind), so a chain of such hops cannot separate them and a check present since the beginning stays invisible to it, while an observation carrying the gate separates them at once (fact_observation_separates).     • CrossIndicesPriceByDatum — Reflection 629 (628's sequel and its correction, in two halves. The KIND is not the price. A class's kind says what SHAPE its obligation has — refutable asks for a guard, mixed asks for a producer plus a discriminator — and those read like different work, which is how "two classes, two prices" got into the plan. What a class COSTS is the DATUM that decides it, and two classes of different kinds can read the same one: §3 counts two kinds and ONE datum among the same three open cells, so the residual is a single obligation that no grouping by kind can see. The trap is that a refutable class always admits SOME refuting guard, so a plausible per-class answer is always in reach, and finding one ends the search before "does a neighbour's datum already cover this?" is asked. A second index re-classifies the first. §4: the same tail is MIXED in one row and NOT A STATE in the other, because the second index constrains reachability — so a kind belongs to a CELL and never to an index value, and reading it off the first index alone makes a settled cell look like open work. Cells handed back that way are free: the refutation here is a guard that shipped nine items earlier, already carried as a FIELD of the invariant. §5 is the arithmetic — crossing doubled the cells (4 → 8) and more than doubled the settled ones (2 → 5) while the residual grew by one cell and shrank to one datum.     • SurrogateEscapeRejected — Reflection 642 (a guard WEAKER than the precondition of the function it guards does not fail on the difference — it fails however that function's TOTAL fallback fails, and when the fallback SUBSTITUTES rather than errors, the difference becomes a set of inputs that are accepted and silently wrong. Char.ofNat is dite n.isValidChar (ofNatAux n) (fun _ => '\0'); both hex-escape decoders guarded on val < 0x110000, which is strictly weaker — the two conditions disagree on exactly the 2048 surrogates U+D800–U+DFFF — so \ud800 took the SUCCESS branch and decoded to NUL, in both pipelines, on input the scanner ACCEPTED, and re-emitted as a: "\0": well-formed YAML asserting a character the author never wrote, and 2048 distinct escapes identified with each other. The repair is not to widen the guard by inspection but to make the guard BE the precondition (Nat.isValidChar itself), which renders the substituting branch unreachable rather than merely unvisited. §2 is why the corpus did not catch it: a lemma HAD been written about this code — char_isValidChar, "every Char is a valid Unicode code point" — and the module docstring cited it as if it established faithful decoding, but it quantifies over the CODOMAIN, so '\0' satisfies it; a lemma about where a function's results live is not a lemma about which result you got, and a total coercion with a silent default is exactly where that stops being pedantic. The faithfulness companion needs the hypothesis the vacuous one lacks (CharClass.toNat_ofNat_of_isValidChar: under the guard the decoded code point IS the requested value), and the indexed twin now carries it as a conjunct of parseHexEscapeIx_decoded. The same file also contradicted itself — a documented U+FFFD "fallback" that no branch implements — which is the tell: prose asserting two incompatible fallbacks means neither was checked. §3 the emitter is structurally immune (escapeChar emits hex only as \xHH for values ≤ 0x1F, so the three 2-digit native_decide bounds re-prove under the stronger guard verbatim), which is why a 2048-code-point hole in the SCANNER cost nothing to close on the emitter side.     • GateOnLandingNotStart — Reflection 643 (an arm whose body reads exactly ONE position has its domain fixed by whatever fact the gate supplies about THAT position — so gating on the position the step STARTED from is a strictly smaller domain bought for nothing, and the repair is not a second body but a second PRODUCER of the same fact plus a wrapper that joins them. So - a⏎- b — the commonest multi-line shape in the language — rode scannerDrop because its pending parks at column 3, while the body that would have composed it was sitting right there, position-generic. §1 proves the gap is coverage, not strength (the start gate implies the landing fact, never conversely, and the witness is exactly the break-crossed park); §2 counts the four cells the two dimensions span — 2 covered before, 3 after, no cell re-attributed; §3 shows the remaining cell is IRREDUCIBLE, a step that crossed nothing from a mid-line park, so the join is complete rather than merely bigger — the test for when to stop looking for producers.     • CoverageNotCallSites — Reflection 645 (an escape hatch's CALL-SITE count is not its coverage. Progress against a deferral, a sorry, or any other escape is a claim about its DOMAIN — which inputs still take it — while what is easy to count is its SITES, how many times it is written; the two are independent in BOTH directions, and §1 exhibits each witness: a copied arm composes strictly more input while the count RISES (count_inverts_on_a_split), and an extracted helper drops the count with nothing composed (count_falls_without_progress). They agree on a MERGE, which is exactly what makes the number feel trustworthy. §2 is the honest measure and its invariance under how the arms are spelled; §3 is the actionable half — when a new case would share an existing arm's body verbatim, WIDEN that arm's gate to a disjunction rather than copying it: identical coverage, fewer sites, and the body authored once (widen_dominates_copy).     • PhantomBranchNotADeferral — Reflection 646 (a branch the runtime cannot take is not a case you owe — and it inflates the escape hatch's stated domain exactly as a real one would. A proof's case split is chosen by the prover; the branches the code can take are chosen by the code, so when the split is finer than the dispatch — a classical by_cases on a character, a cases on a constructor no producer builds — the extra branches are PHANTOMS, and routing them to a deferral makes them read as deferred INPUT. The repair is one lemma read off the dispatcher, applied at every phantom site at once: scanNextToken_dispatchBlockIndicators opens each of its three arms with its own literal test and falls through to none, so a .ok (some s') result NAMES the character (indicator_of_some), and exhausted is the three-way contradiction the four block-dispatch lemmas discharge with. §2 is the point that the branch has nothing to build because it has no input to build it for. §3 is the measurement this forces on [[CoverageNotCallSites]]: refuting a phantom drops the stated domain AND the site count — a merge's signature exactly — while composing nothing, so R645's two measures cannot separate refute from compose (stated_domain_cannot_separate_them) and the honest measure must be the REACHABLE domain (reachable_domain_separates_them, refute_composed_nothing). A third signature appears with it: re-gating so a real family is handled by an existing arm's route moves the reachable domain and leaves the sites alone (fold_moves_it_without_moving_sites) — item 21's landing gate on accum_block_on_noPending, the last of the four still gating on the PARK column rather than where the step lands ([[GateOnLandingNotStart]]).     • EqualityGateHidesTwoOrders — Reflection 656 (an EQUALITY gate on an ORDERED index hides two constructs, and one of them is usually already written next door. k ≠ n reads like one condition, costs one by_cases and routes to one escape with one argument list — every surface says "one case" — but the negation of an equality on an ordered set is asymmetric, and if the index parameterizes a production whose own parameter is a DIFFERENCE, the two orders disagree about whether that difference exists at all. Split before pricing: the split is Nat.lt_or_ge and the side condition each half needs is the SAME arithmetic that decides which construct it is, so it arrives free rather than having to be found ([[SideConditionNeedsItsQuantity]] is the case where it does not) — difference_exists_iff beside composable_half_needs_nothing_new. Truncating subtraction is what hides it: extra n k is total, so the wrap still elaborates on the wrong side and silently names another collection (truncation_is_silent). Then check the SIBLING arms before writing a body: an escape arm's neighbours in the same case split are the first place its body is likely to be sitting, and an arm that defers on a condition the others never test is a bug report rather than a design (the_asymmetry_is_the_report, sibling_already_covers_it, delegation_is_the_siblings_body which is rfl) — so the cases behind one escape do not cost the same and the item's price is not their number (cost_is_not_uniform). Finally the split's ARITY belongs to the STATE, not to the gate: the same gate splits 2-and-1 across two lemmas because the nested reading needs a node-shaped hole and only one of the two pendings has one (awaiting_admits_nested, completed_admits_neither, arity_differs), and what the dedent lacks is a FRAME rather than strength (dedent_wants_a_frame).     • VacuityIsAClaimAboutTheRuntime — Reflection 657 ("expected VACUOUS" is a claim about the RUNTIME, not about the proof, and it is cheap to test: build the input the branch describes and run it. Either it is refused, and the vacuity claim is a lemma waiting to be written, or it PARSES, and the branch was never proof debt — it was a bug report the deferral had been carrying ([[PhantomBranchNotADeferral]] is the case where the claim holds and the phantoms retire; this is the case where it does not, and one experiment separates them — vacuity_claim_is_false, branch_was_reachable). What makes the claim look safe is that it IS true of the arm somebody checked: a guard implemented on one sibling reads as a guard on the family, the named runtime error genuinely exists, and every casually chosen input lands on the checked arm (guard_is_not_on_every_sibling, family_looks_guarded_from_one_arm). That is [[EqualityGateHidesTwoOrders]] §2's asymmetry pointed the other way — there an arm DEFERRED on a condition its siblings never tested and the body was next door; here an arm CHECKS one its siblings never check, and what is next door is the hole — same sweep, different currency: a line of delegation versus a shipped over-acceptance. §3 is the prerequisite: check what the branch's STATEMENT says against what its proof knows, because a disjunct weakened past its own witness ('\t' ∈ s.chars — a tab ANYWHERE downstream — where the induction knew it was inside the run just walked) is unrefutable at any price, and restoring it is free, the same induction already visits the witness (weakened_disjunct_is_unrefutable, located_disjunct_is_refutable, locating_costs_nothing). §4 is the boundary the repair must respect: whether whitespace IS indentation depends on what FOLLOWS it, so a blunt rule over-rejects and the check belongs where the construct is recognised, anchored at the ENTRY's first character rather than the indicator's (same_prefix_two_verdicts, blunt_rule_over_rejects, entry_start_decides).     • GuardInTheWrongCoordinate — Reflection 658 (a runtime guard the proof cannot reach is usually a guard stated in the wrong COORDINATE, not a coupling that is expensive. A check keyed on what some derived state machine recorded — a saved position, a cached index, a flag — is reachable from a proof only through an INVARIANT about that value, and when no such invariant exists, building one is a separate item; a check keyed on the coordinate the SPECIFICATION names is reachable from what the proof already carries (key_guard_depends_on_the_state, guards_differ_without_the_invariant, and verdicts_agree_on_indentation for the domain where they coincide).     • RefusedLandingsFundTheReading — Reflection 679 (a runtime floor check inside a scan loop is not only a refutation device — once it covers EVERY landing, it is the production's own justification at the checked index: the same induction that builds the 0-reading builds the reading at n, with the check's negation as the only new fact per landing (checked_reads_at).
